# Fudge register

Alex, 19 Nov 2026: "the physics is WAY more interesting than faking it. But understand a browser has limited
capability. Fall on physics rather than simplification where practical. If we decide to "fudge" it is a known and
documented fudge with a damn fine reason to exist."

A fudge is any deliberate simplification, stand-in, tuned constant or placeholder that is not the published
physics. Each one is listed here, in the PR that adds it, by the thread that adds it.

Columns: **where** (file and item), **what it does instead of the physics**, **why** (the damn fine reason:
browser cost, missing data, not built yet, tuned to match an observation), **real physics that would replace it**,
**status** (stand-in until package X, permanent with reason, to review).

Add entries under your area. Keep one row per fudge.

## Orbits and formation

### Deliberate simplifications, stand-ins, tuned constants and placeholders

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why (as the code or log says) | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/sse-kernel/src/orbits.rs` (`orbits_at`) | Two-body Kepler orbits per parent, fixed elements: no perturbations, precession, tides or resonance libration | Chart and 3D placement need any time in microseconds on a phone; the F-track n-body run is for formation, not playback | Secular perturbation theory (Laplace-Lagrange) for slow element drift, or replay of a stored n-body ephemeris for systems the GM marks as interacting | stand-in, permanent for playback with reason |
| `orbits.rs` phase | Bodies with no stored mean anomaly get a keyed random phase (stream `orbit.phase.v1`) | V3 saves and generated systems often carry no epoch phase | None needed when a phase is authored or imported; the draw is labelled `phase_source: drawn` | documented |
| disc.rs:418-421 | Initial disc mass 0.03 M* ± 0.4 dex, capped at 0.15; rc0 30 AU ± 0.3 dex | "Young (class 0/I) discs", Andrews 2010/2013 | Draw from published Class 0/I or II disc-mass and radius distributions as functions of M* (e.g. Andrews 2013's M_d ∝ M*^1.0 to 1.5 with 0.7 dex scatter; Tobin et al. 2020 VANDAM) | tuned, documented |
| disc.rs:445 | alpha = 0.01 for every disc | none stated | Draw alpha (1e-4 to 1e-2), or use a wind-driven disc. Alpha also sets the isolation mass and the gap K. | undocumented constant |
| disc.rs:448 | kappa = 3 cm^2/g Rosseland mean, scaled by dust | none stated | Bell & Lin 1994 opacity table κ(T, ρ) | undocumented constant |
| disc.rs:212-219 | Viscous time from the irradiation temperature at rc0 only (no accretion heating) | first order | Integrate ν(r) with the full midplane temperature | simplification |
| disc.rs:437-441 | X-ray photoevaporation 1e-9 × M* M_sun/yr; external FUV 1e-9 (G0/1000)(rc/30) | "sketch, after Haworth et al. 2018" | OCE12's Mdot_X ≈ 6.25e-9 (M*)^-0.068 (L_X/1e30)^1.14 (unverified), with L_X drawn per star. Interpolate the FRIED grid for the external part. The internal rate is probably about 6× low (unverified). | documented sketch |
| disc.rs:225-226 | Disc clears instantly when Mdot falls to Mdot_pe; t_disperse at least 0.1 Myr | two-timescale model | Inside-out clearing with a gap at r_g, then the outer disc removed (Clarke, Gendrin & Sotomayor 2001; Owen 2012) | simplification |
| disc.rs:45-51, 79-87 | Condensation as sharp steps at single temperatures (pressure-independent) | standard first order | Pressure-dependent sublimation T (e.g. T_H2O ∝ ln P), partial condensation sequence (Lodders 2003 tables) | simplification |
| disc.rs:95 | SOLAR_MIX split of Z by carrier | "split by carrier as in Oberg 2011" | Derive from Lodders 2003 (or Asplund 2009) elemental abundances with an explicit carrier allocation | numbers unsourced individually |
| disc.rs:101-115 | Water left = 1 - 0.6 (C/O - 0.55), clamped | "Oberg 2011 figure 2, first order" | Oxygen budget bookkeeping: O to silicates, then CO, CO2, the rest to H2O | first-order fit |
| disc.rs:426-433; F1.md:22 | Companion under 2 AU gives a circumbinary disc with cavity 2.5 a; else truncation at peri/3 | AL94 | AL94 or Miranda & Lai 2015 truncation radius as a function of q and e | simplification (values inside the published range) |
| inherit.rs:85, 136 | Z = 0.02 × 10^[Fe/H] | none (Hurley-fit convention) | One solar Z for the whole chain (0.0142 or 0.0149), with Hurley fits rescaled | **inconsistent with disc.rs's 0.0142** |
| growth.rs:38-41 | Pebble flux = Z × gas Mdot (PEBBLE_FLUX_PER_Z 1.0; F3.md says 1.5) | LJ14 order of magnitude | Dust growth and drift model (Birnstiel, Klahr & Ercolano 2012 two-population; LJ14 eq. for the pebble front) giving a time-dependent flux | tuned; **doc/code mismatch** |
| growth.rs:42-43 | Stokes number fixed at 0.1 | standard | St from the drift or fragmentation limit (Birnstiel 2012) | constant |
| growth.rs:317-322 | eta = h^2 (pressure-gradient term dropped) | none | eta = -(1/2) h^2 dlnP/dlnr from the disc profile (about 1.36 h^2 for this disc) | simplification, about 30 percent |
| growth.rs:307-313 | Isolation mass without eq. 26's diffusion term; M* scaling extrapolated | "pressure factor near 1" | Full Bitsch 2018 eq. 26 (see fix P2) | **omission (fix P2)** |
| growth.rs:324-329 | LC_COEFF 0.02 for the pebble-phase envelope | "Lee and Chiang 2015, scaled" (F3.md: calibrated) | LC15's analytic GCR with opacity, temperature and metallicity terms | tuned |
| growth.rs:331-336 | KH_COEFF_YR 1e11, outside the published 1e8 to 1e10 | "dusty envelopes run slower" | Ikoma 2000 or Ida & Lin 2004 with an opacity-dependent k1 | tuned outside the range; **REF string wrong (fix M4)** |
| growth.rs:45-46 | TYPE_I_REDUCTION 0.5 | "Ida and Lin 2008's C_I" | Full Paardekooper et al. 2011 torque (Lindblad plus saturating corotation) | tuned; **REF says 0.1 (fix M5)** |
| growth.rs:47-48 | TYPE_II_REDUCTION 0.02 | calibrated on hot-Jupiter rarity (Wright 2012) | Kanagawa 2018 alone, plus inner-disc gas depletion (Lee & Chiang 2016; F3 known limits) | tuned |
| growth.rs:49-50, 672-679 | Water-line trap for 3 to 15 M_E | Hasegawa & Pudritz 2011 | Compute the corotation torque sign from the local dlnΣ/dlnr and dlnT/dlnr (Paardekooper 2011) | tuned |
| growth.rs:51-52, 594-597 | Giant takes up to 80 percent of the disc flow | Lubow & D'Angelo 2006 | as published (value unverified) | constant |

| growth.rs:445-455 | Resonance ratio drawn (25 percent 2:1, 50 percent 3:2, 25 percent 4:3) | none | Capture by migration rate against the resonance width (e.g. Ogihara & Kobayashi 2013; Batygin 2015 capture criterion) | **unsourced draw** |
| growth.rs:700-723 | Embryos closer than 2 sqrt(3) R_H,m merge at once, mass-weighted a | Gladman 1993 | That limit is Hill stability, not a merge criterion. | crates/sse-surface/src/core.rs `CoreSettings::melt_depression_per_light` = 1.0, `light_fraction` = 0.1, Mars `core_light_fraction` 0.3 (fixture) | Core alloy melting point as pure iron's (Anzellini et al. 2013) times (1 − 1.0 × light fraction) | No iron-alloy melting rows yet; the linear drop is set so Earth's inner core is born 0.5 to 1.5 Gyr ago and Mars's sulphur-rich core stays liquid (InSight) | Iron alloy liquidus by composition (Fe-S, Fe-O, Fe-Si) from MP21's iron wave | Stand-in until MP21 (MF1) |
| core.rs `nucleation_undercooling_k` = 150 | The centre must cool 150 K below melting before an inner core first freezes; it then jumps to the size the melting curve allows | Homogeneous nucleation needs hundreds of kelvin (Huguet et al. 2018), impurities less; no settled value. With the jump Earth's inner core today is about 3,000 km against the real 1,221 km | A nucleation model with the core's impurity content; partial jump with latent heat in time | To review (MF1) |
| core.rs `dry_viscosity_factor` = 100, `degas_gyr` = 1 | A dry mantle is 100 times stiffer than a wet one; every mantle starts wet and loses its water with a 1 Gyr e-folding under a stagnant lid, regaining it under moving plates | Water weakens olivine by 10 to 100 times (Hirth and Kohlstedt 2003); mantle water is not tracked. This is what kills Venus's and Mars's fields while Earth's runs | Mantle water budget: degassing by melting, regassing by subduction (e.g. Sandu et al. 2011) | Stand-in until a mantle water budget (MF1) |
| core.rs `lower_mantle_factor` = 100, `t_mantle_max_k` = 2200, `core_superheat_k` = 400 | Lower mantle 100 times stiffer than the upper; mantle potential temperature capped at 2,200 K (melting vents the rest); the core starts 400 K above the mantle liquidus at its top (Andrault et al. 2011) | Parametrised convection (Driscoll and Bercovici 2014) needs these; 100 puts Earth's lower mantle near 10²² Pa s and its core heat flow today at about 8 TW | Pressure-dependent viscosity with activation volume; a melt and heat-pipe model; accretion energy for the core's start | Calibrated (MF1) |
| core.rs `mg_per_k` = 1e-5, `mg_floor_k` = 3,000 K | Magnesium oxide comes out of the core at a fixed rate per kelvin of cooling while the core top is above 3,000 K | O'Rourke and Stevenson 2016 give the rate; what dissolved at formation depends on the giant impacts, which are not tracked | Mg partition from formation temperature (Badro et al. 2016) | Stand-in (MF1) |

Document that the body is assumed to form at its star's birth (Myr offsets ignored) | undocumented, harmless |

At least state that the first ~0.4 Gyr is extrapolated (O'Brien et al. 2014) | documented in the S4 log; validity gap not documented |

## Climate, weather and life

### Fudge register

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why (as the code or log says) | Real physics to replace it | Status |

| F1. `feedback.rs:183-187`, `history.rs:523-527` | O₂ burial is 0.1% of each metabolism's **energy cap**, and nothing on O₂ pushes back except √O₂ weathering | L2.md:36: "nothing in the box yet raises oxidative weathering or fires to match" | Feed realised production (see fixes P3). Burial efficiency falls with O₂ through phosphorus retention (Van Cappellen and Ingall 1996). Fire caps land biomass above about 25% O₂ (Lenton and Watson 2000; Belcher and McElwain 2008). Use the COPSE functional forms (Bergman et al. 2004). | **Undocumented error, not a fudge.** It explains the 51% O₂: land adds 42 Gt C/yr to an ocean already counted at its 118 Gt light cap instead of its 38 Gt realised production. | F34. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/star_tree.rs` (Y1) | The count tree's coarse nodes take their expected systems from the envelope averaged over four Gauss-Legendre heights at the node's centre in the plane, not over the whole node; the 10 pc cells use G5's own centre value. A split's binomial is a rounded normal once its variance passes 25. Each root block's class shares come from a fixed quadrature (512 IMF points, finest in the bright tail, by 48 log-age bins of the birth history at the block's radius), so the same shares hold across the block. Fourteen luminosity classes (0.1, 1, 3, 10, 30, 100 L☉, then half decades to 300,000 L☉; the 1 to 10 and 10 to 100 decades split in the Y2 fix so the commonest naked-eye stars search less space). A leaf draws each class with its own keyed slot (`j × classes + c`), so one class is drawn without counting the others; white dwarfs and giants fall in whichever class their luminosity gives. A leaf searches only above the lowest mass and below the greatest age the quadrature found in its class, with two IMF steps and one age bin of margin. Fill frames are 0.1 Myr, so a system drifts under 5 pc from the cell that drew it | Reading the envelope at every point of a 640 pc block costs as much as drawing it, which is what the tree avoids | Node means from the envelope's own vertical integral; exact binomials by inversion for every count; per-node class shares where the birth history changes across a block | starting set |
| F35. `crates/sse-kernel/src/sky.rs` and `crates/sse-cosmos/src/sky.rs` (Y2) | The sky draw: each class is walked out to the distance where a dust-free blackbody of its top luminosity (the brightest V over 24 temperatures from 2,000 to 50,000 K) reaches the limit, plus 0.5 mag, capped at 4 kpc (`max_radius_pc`), so stars beyond 4 kpc never show; the top class's brightest member is 1,000,000 L☉ (`top_lsun`); each star is a sum of blackbody components (no line blanketing or bolometric corrections); dust A_V is the envelope's gas column along a straight line (24 steps) times 10^[Fe/H], over 21 M☉ pc⁻² per magnitude (Güver and Özel 2009 with helium), with no clumps or clouds and R_V 3.1 everywhere; the fill is drawn for the observer's frame (design ruling) while each star's place and light are read at the time its light left; pins drawn from a reached cell replace their slot, every other pin is added | Sky counts from the Sun's place land within about 25 per cent of the real ones from V 2 to 6.5 (tested within a factor of 1.5 at 3, 4 and 5), and a 6.5 sky costs seconds, not minutes | Model atmospheres (Castelli and Kurucz) for the bands; a dust radius per class from the least dust along any line; clumpy dust from the O9 patch model once it carries 3D clouds; walk out to where the top class really fades | starting set |
| F36. `crates/sse-sky/src/glow.rs` and `crates/sse-cosmos/src/glow_mix.rs` (Y3) | The glow map: each pixel's sight line is 96 log-spaced steps from 1 pc to 30 kpc, read at the line's centre only (no average over the pixel); the light mix is the count tree's quadrature (768 IMF points by 48 log-age bins) at the step's galactocentric radius in 0.25 kpc bins, so metallicity and age change only with radius; each point is one blackbody (no line blanketing) read at the band's effective wavelength (as Y2's stand-in, so a 10,000 K star reads B−V 0.14); companions are left out of the light; the dust is the draw's own column (gas times 10^[Fe/H], 21 M☉ pc⁻² per magnitude of A_V) at R_V 3.1 everywhere, with no scattered light; the default observer is the Sun's radius in the plane | A full star-by-star sum is impossible and the draw's own rules must be reused exactly so the glow and the points add to the whole sky | Pixel-averaged lines (several per pixel at nside 32), the light mix by height as well as radius, companion light from the binary fraction and mass-ratio law, passband-integrated spectra, dust that scatters (the diffuse galactic light, Brandt and Draine 2012, doi:10.1088/0004-637X/744/2/129) and R_V that varies along the line | Stand-in; checked against the polar sky (V 24.1 against about 23 to 24 mag/arcsec² measured, Leinert et al. 1998, doi:10.1051/aas:1998105) |

| F43. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/assembly.rs` `tree` (GH1) | One assembly tree per halo: G2's major mergers are pinned (the smaller progenitor holding ξ/(1+ξ) of the descendant), then the unevolved subhalo mass function (Giocoli et al. 2008) fills bins of ψ top down with keyed Poisson counts, each bin's mean less the pins in it; infall when the host held a share of its mass uniform above ψ; a member is kept only if it leaves room for the mass expected under it down to ψ 10⁻⁵ (closure), so a host already built by its mergers takes fewer; a progenitor's own halo is the median halo with its infall mass at that time; no smooth-accretion term, no sub-subhaloes carried with a progenitor | A tree per halo for a browser, the same members at every depth, without running extended Press-Schechter splits | A Parkinson, Cole and Helly (2008) split tree conditioned on G2's mergers, with sub-subhaloes and smooth accretion | Checked: about 52 members above 10⁻³ for 10¹² M☉ (55 expected before closure), 96 % of the expected count above 10⁻⁵; trees whose G2 mergers sum near the whole host (2 in 30) keep under 10 Chart members |

| F46. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/infall.rs` (GH2) | After infall, each progenitor is one test body in the host's spherically averaged potential (DM4's curve; the disc counts only for its passes), entering on r200 with a speed drawn from 0.9 to 1.4 V200 and a radial share from 0.3 to 1 (Jiang et al. 2015's range, not its full distribution). Drag: Chandrasekhar with ln Λ = ln(1 + M/m) and σ = v_c/√2 (isothermal). Stripping: the mass outside the tidal radius of its infall NFW profile (Dutton and Macciò 2014 concentration) goes at α = 0.55 per dynamical time (SatGen's fitted number), sped by disc heat; stars (GH4) leave at the same rate as the dark matter once outside the tidal radius, the share inside it being an exponential disc's 1 − (1 + x) e^(−x) at x = r_t / R_d of the satellite's own disc scale, and never return (an exponential, not the measured tidal tracks); a body with no galaxy keeps the old rule, stars staying until a tenth of the dark matter is left. Ram pressure against 2πGΣ_tot Σ_gas at the disc scale, all or nothing. Disc shocks impulsive. Torn apart below a thousandth of its infall mass or at heat 1; reached the centre inside 0.01 r200 | Satellites that sink, are stripped and stop forming stars by their own orbits, cheap enough for Survey level (0.8 s natively for 2,140 bodies) | Tidal tracks for stars (Errani et al. 2022), a profile that changes shape as it is stripped (Green and van den Bosch 2019), the host's disc as a flattened potential, the satellites' pull on the host (GH3) | 23 Nov |
| F47. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/feed.rs` (GH3) | A galaxy that is looked at (the galaxy chart, the home galaxy) is fed by its assembly tree at the Chart level (ψ ≥ 10⁻³): each member's own G3 galaxy and GH2 orbit decide what arrives and when (stars lost on the way to the stellar halo, gas pushed out to the hot reservoir, the rest with its black hole at the centre). The members' own galaxies are fed by nothing (recursion one level deep), their orbits run in the unfed host's potential (one pass), and their stars and gas are read at their own G3 runs as if isolated until their star formation stops. Responses by ratios: bulge growth = thin disc × μ × (1 − gas share) (Hopkins et al. 2009); disc heating = 0.1 × satellite mass over disc mass at a merger and 0.02 × the same × v_c/v_z at a disc pass (after Toth and Ostriker 1992; the two numbers set so a Milky-Way-mass host keeps a thick disc of a tenth to a third of its disc); G3's burst at mergers, none at passes; a merger's stars all go to the bulge. Marks: strength x/(x + half) of a driving ratio per kind, fading by e over a fixed time per kind (design 9.4's "Lasts" column); shapes are a few numbers. A leading arm (design 9.4) is driven by the backwards share of a closest approach or meal × a mass term (full at μ = 0.01, falling as 1/(1 + (μ/0.1)²)) × exp(−pericentre / 0.4 disc scale lengths) × how far the halo outweighs the disc inside the pass (none at 2, full at 8), all from one set of 2D, in-plane runs of NGC 4622 (Byrd, Freeman and Howard 1993); it fades by e in 0.9 Gyr (a third left after a Gyr, about three rotations of the disc's edge) rather than by the disc's own rotation; checked against the 1-in-100 yardstick (Iye et al. 2019). Below ψ = 10⁻³ nothing arrives (the halo's smooth growth counts it). Inked mergers keep G3's ratio rule | A tree member is a few milliseconds; Survey depth (2,000 members) and a feed for every galaxy in the sky would cost seconds | Feed each member from its own tree (recursion by looking), orbit in the fed potential, response shapes from disc simulations (Laporte et al. 2018; Hopkins et al. 2009 in full); debris placed by orbit is GH4 (F49) | stand-in |
| F48. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/ledger.rs` (DM3) | A cell's ordinary matter by reservoir reads G3 run on a median halo once per half dex of halo mass, by each halo's mass rather than its own history; haloes below the catalogue floor are counted by the mean conditional mass function down to 10⁶ M☉ and those under 10⁹ M☉ hold no galaxy; environments on 8³ equal-mass parcels of the T-web at the truncated Zel'dovich smoothing, each weighted by its Zel'dovich room for the share of space (cosmic web PR) | Running G3 on each of a cell's hundreds of haloes would take seconds | Each catalogued halo's own G3 run (or its fed galaxy where looked at); a cooling-based floor for galaxies; a multiscale classifier (NEXUS+) beside the T-web | stand-in |
| F49. `crates/sse-cosmos/src/remains.rs` (GH4) | What a member lost becomes a component: stars lost less than a mixing time ago lie along its stored path (equal Gaussian blobs over a half length of spread × time since they left, at most half a period, width spread × r with spread = (m/M(<r))^(1/3) at its last close pass, capped at 1; Johnston 1998), or for closest over farthest approach under 0.1 in a cap at the turning point; older ones are a spherical cloud ∝ 1/r² between the loss-weighted nearest and farthest distance, flat inside, falling as e^(−(r − apo)/0.2 apo) beyond. The mixing time is half a period over the spread, at most GH2's 5 Gyr. Path points every 0.02 Gyr, past its end it repeats with the period. Births from the member's own rings (4 rings, 40 time bins), its stars' [Fe/H] the bin's mean with the rings' spread. Halo stars move on G5's closed-form epicycle with halo reach: radial amplitude half the debris' span, vertical up to half its farthest distance, turning at half the circular speed times its galaxy's prograde share. The glow lights them with the disc's light mix at that radius. ### The 51% oxygen gap

No misremembered constant explains it. The constants that set the steady state check out against Catling and Claire 2005, which I read: reductants 3e12, an O₂ inventory of 3.8e19 mol, and a burial share of about 0.1% of NPP.

The cause is **F1 (fixes P3)**. The air box is fed each metabolism's energy cap rather than its realised production:
- The ocean's oxygenic input is 118 Gt C/yr (the light cap) instead of 38 Gt C/yr (nutrient-limited production). Earth's 21% before land therefore comes from an error.
- Land adds its real 42 Gt C/yr on top, giving 160 Gt C/yr: burial 1.33e13, minus reductants 3.1e12, then (1.02e13/6.5e12)² × 0.2095 = **0.518**.

Two smaller physics errors (CH₄ lifetime 9.6 → 9.1 yr; escape coefficient ×2 high) do not affect the oxic steady state.

## Galaxy and cosmology

### Fudge register (deliberate simplifications, stand-ins, tuned constants, placeholders)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why (as code/log says) | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/sse-cosmos/src/nucleus.rs` merger delay | Two black holes coalesce a fixed 1 Gyr after their galaxies merge | No dynamics of the pair are modelled | Dynamical friction (Chandrasekhar), stellar hardening and gas-driven inspiral; delays from 0.1 Gyr to never (final parsec) | sketch (G-nuclei.md) |
| `nucleus.rs` seeds | Direct collapse: a keyed 10% chance at one redshift (z = 10) in atomic-cooling haloes | Needs Lyman-Werner fields and metal-free gas, which are not modelled | Seed from the halo's own history: metal enrichment and a nearby star-forming neighbour (Lyman-Werner flux) | sketch |
| `nucleus.rs` radio loudness | Keyed once for the whole life from the Best et al. 2005 fit | Jets need an accretion-state model | Jet power from the accretion state (hard or soft, Eddington ratio) and spin, switching with episodes | stand-in |
| `nucleus.rs` light | One radiative efficiency (0.1) for every hole; L ∝ ṁ² below 1% Eddington | Spin is not tracked; the λ² law is the standard ADAF scaling, source passage not yet read | Spin evolution with efficiency 0.057 to 0.42; the RIAF efficiency curve (Yuan and Narayan 2014) | documented |
| `crates/sse-cosmos/src/galaxy.rs` `exsitu_black_hole` | Merging galaxies' holes arrive at 1e-3 of their stars | G3 does not follow satellites' own holes | Each satellite's own hole from its own (smaller) galaxy run | documented, editable param |

Voids then hold about 35 to 45 percent of the matter, against the measured 15 percent: one Zel'dovich step at σ ≈ 1 is too linear to empty them further | measured share of space as the one editable number; no tuning of the threshold itself | A multiscale classifier (NEXUS+, Cautun et al. 2013) on a 2LPT or N-body-calibrated field, which would give the matter share as well | documented |

Haloes start where G2 puts them, the highest of a few keyed peaks in their 8 Mpc cell, and only those that started inside the cube are drawn (as with the parcels). Galaxies in voids come out about 28% of them (voids hold too much matter, row above) | running G3 per halo for ten thousand haloes is minutes, the median per half dex is milliseconds | Each halo's own G3 history (what the galaxy chart runs); a conditional luminosity function by environment (void galaxies bluer and fainter, Hoyle et al. 2005) | documented |

| `crates/sse-nav/src/entry.rs` chances | Chance it goes wrong is a logistic in figure over limit, half at the limit, width 0.1 (`nav.entry.harm_width`); the corridor chance is a normal spread of the pass height by 0.25 scale heights (`pass_sigma_scale_heights`) | The GM wants a rough chance beside each danger; nothing here is rolled | Failure curves per part (shield test data, crew injury criteria such as the Brinkley dynamic response model) and a navigation error from the ship's sensors and ephemeris | stand-in, the basis is sent with every chance |
| `crates/sse-nav/src/entry.rs` powered landing and take-off | Δv is a low orbit's speed plus g × burn time × 0.5 (`ascent_gravity_share`); drag not counted | A burn's cost at any thrust without flying an ascent | A gravity-turn ascent integrated with drag (the descent's RK4 run backwards) | stand-in |
| `crates/sse-nav/src/entry.rs` hull without numbers | A ship with no `aero` is a sphere of 6.67 m³ to the tonne (150 kg/m³) with C_d 1.2; no `shield` means the bare hull's flux limit (`flux_limit_w_m2`); no gear means 1 m/s touchdown | Stock ships and the GM's own carry no shapes until the renderer's designer writes them | The designer's numbers (shield, aero, landing) for every ship | stand-in until ships carry shapes; the answer says when a shape is guessed |
| `packs/base-nav` `nav.entry.brief_g` | Brief crew tolerance read roughly off Eiband 1959's eyeballs-in curve (25 g for 1 s to 4 g for 1,000 s), never under the ship's hours limit | A published curve, read by eye | The tabulated NASA-STD-3001 sustained acceleration limits by axis, and the posture the crew sits in | values to verify |
| `sse-kernel/src/cosmos.rs` `galaxy_chart` halo | The median halo for a named mass, not the campaign's own galaxy | G7 (Milky Way pack, campaign galaxy) not built | The campaign's galaxy placed by G7 | stand-in until G7 |

## Light, rendering and sound

Rule from the owner: fall back on physics where practical. A fudge has to be known, documented, and have a good reason to exist.

### 1. Fudges

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| renderer `gpu-shade.ts` `STAR_FALLBACK` (EC1, "Shadows in True") | Where a view gives no star distance (the size comparison, the class gallery), shadows' soft edges are drawn for a star 0.00465 rad across (the Sun from Earth) | those views draw a body alone, with no star placed | the star's real angular size from the world's own orbit, passed by every view | Stand-in |
| renderer `gpu-shade.ts` (EC1) | Shadows are cast by spheres: a flattened world's (Saturn's 10%) and a small body's own shape are not. The ambient trace is not shadowed. The rings cast no penumbra (the star's size is left out at the ring), and moons cast none on rings | the casters' figures are not passed to the shader; the ambient trace stands for light from everywhere, not the star | the caster's spheroid in the shader; penumbra through the rings; moons on the rings | Stand-in |
| renderer `eclipse.ts` `GLINT_FALLBACK` (EC1, EC2) | Where the kernel has not answered for a world (no sea it knows of, a liquid without an index on the sheet, the answer not read yet), its glint is drawn at the kernel's own answer for water under a 7 m/s wind: slope variance 0.0388, reflectance 0.0204 | a world drawn before its weather is read still needs a glint | the weather op's `glint` for that world, which replaces it as soon as it is read | Stand-in |
| kernel `sse.light.sea_glint`, `sea::glint` (EC2) | Cox and Munk's slope fit, made on Earth's seas with the wind at 12.5 m, is read at the 10 m wind and on every liquid as it stands; the wind is scaled by the air-to-liquid density as the waves are (`drive`), and the slope variance is the year's mean over each band's winds, weighted by sea and area, not today's | the only measured slope law; no fit exists for methane seas or other gravities | a capillary-gravity slope spectrum from the liquid's surface tension and density (the waves' own `trains` already carry the dispersion) | Stand-in |
| views `system-3d/bodies.ts` `placedBodies` (EC1) | A body the kernel has not placed by date stands where its distance and phase put it in its parent's plane (no inclination) when its shadow is worked out | a fixture or a view without orbits_at has only the phase | the kernel's orbits_at places every body (it does where a session is open) | Stand-in |
| renderer `plumes.ts` (TRUE2) | Grains scatter by Henyey-Greenstein with g = 0.7, the same for every plume | the light thread's phase table per particle kind (from grain size and the sheet's refractive index, ask K-e) is not built yet; the Jendersie and d'Eon fit holds only for 5 to 50 µm water droplets | that phase table | stand-in until K-e |
| renderer `plumes.ts` `PLUME_GRAINS`, `PLUME_VENTS`, `JET_SPREAD` | 1800 points at strength 1 from 24 vents, each jet a 13° cone, launch speeds ±40% about the kernel's grain speed | a drawing's count; the kernel gives the cap, speeds, height and strength, not the jets | jet places and tilts from the kernel's fractures (tiger stripes), a grain size and speed distribution | permanent as a drawing, numbers from the kernel |
| renderer `hot-glow.ts` (TRUE3) | A world's glow is its blackbody at one temperature over its whole disc, at the day side's exposure | the kernel gives one temperature per world; hot spots (Io's lava) are not yet temperatures on the tiles | per-tile temperatures (lava lakes, a hot Jupiter's day-night contrast) through the detail ladder (VP-P1) | stand-in |
| `ui-contract/src/light.ts:327-373` `describeLight` (stars) | A star is a pure blackbody at Teff: no absorption lines, line blanketing, limb darkening, chromosphere or corona. | The kernel publishes no light facts yet. A blackbody gets the colour right to a few per cent for FGK stars. | A model-atmosphere spectrum per star (a results-as-data grid such as PHOENIX or ATLAS, by Teff, log g and [Fe/H]) as a kernel light-description block. Corona and activity come from design 4A (O10). | documented ("Stubbed" in the why text and the O1 log) |
| `light.ts:355-366` (planets, reflected light) | Grey reflectance: the star's spectrum times one Bond albedo. Full phase is assumed, and the dilution factor is wrong (/4; see fixes 2.1). | There is no reflectance spectrum or phase angle in the scene facts. | A wavelength-dependent reflectance from the surface and atmosphere (Rayleigh scattering, cloud and haze, surface minerals, ice, vegetation red edge), a phase function from the observer geometry, and geometric albedo p(λ). | documented as stubbed; **the factor is a bug, not a fudge** |
| `light.ts:367-369` (planet heat) | Thermal emission at one `surface_k`, with emissivity 1 − Bond albedo. | There is no temperature map or atmosphere emission yet. | Emission from the atmosphere's emitting layers (brightness temperature by band; CO₂ and H₂O bands), with day and night sides. | documented as stubbed |
| `light.ts:407-420` `teffFromSpectral` | A hand table of class temperatures (O 41000 … L 2100 K), linear in subclass, used for players' scenes. Luminosity class is ignored, so a K1 III giant gets a dwarf's Teff. | Players are told a spectral type, not Teff. | A citable table (e.g. Pecaut and Mamajek 2013, from memory and to be verified) shipped as pack data, by luminosity class. Better still, a disclosed Teff fact from the kernel. | labelled "Stubbed", no source; **needs a source** |
| `light.ts:255,287` camera white | The camera is white-balanced to a 6504 K Planckian, not the CIE D65 spectrum. | A blackbody needs no table, and D65's correlated temperature is 6504 K. | The tabulated CIE D65 spectral power distribution (300–830 nm), since D65 sits off the Planckian locus. The difference is a slight tint. | in a code comment; not in the recipe legend |
| `light.recipes.json:14-16` Camera recipe | Box-car RGB filters (400–490, 490–580, 590–700 nm) and saturation ×1.6. | It stands for "a camera and a press stretch", and the legend says so (`actual-enhanced`). | Measured sensor or filter response curves (for example a named camera or the HST/JWST filter set) as pack data. | documented in the recipe legend |
| `light.ts:61` `NARROW_M` | Every narrowband filter is a 3 nm box centred on one line. [S II] at 671.6 nm loses most of its 673.1 nm partner. | It keeps one width for all lines. | Per-filter centre and width (or transmission curves) as recipe data. Treat [S II] as a doublet. | in a comment ("roughly"); the doublet is not mentioned |
| `light.ts:252-283` Draper cut | Any purely thermal source below 798 K is shown as no colour in visible recipes. | Chromaticity normalises brightness away, so without the cut a 300 K world would show as deep red. | A visibility test on absolute brightness: visible-band luminance at the observer against the eye's threshold (photopic and scotopic), after distance. This needs the observer geometry that O9 sensing brings. | documented (comment and O1 log); good reason |
| `light.ts:161-166` `chromaticity` | Colour only: the brightest channel is set to 1 and out-of-gamut channels are clipped at 0. | Brightness spans many decades, which a mark cannot show. | Exposure and tone mapping with a stated exposure (design 2.3), and gamut mapping rather than clipping. | documented in the doc comment |
| `light.ts:262-276` single-band floor | Light more than 30 decades below the brightest visible light is dropped, and the ramp spans the recipe's `decades`. | This is a display range. | Instrument sensitivity and noise floor from the sensor model (design 5A / O9). | documented in a comment |
| `light.ts:65-75` lines | No body has emission lines yet (`lines: []`), so the SHO and HOO recipes show only continuum. | Nebulae are not built yet (O3, O4). | A line-strength grid from Cloudy and MAPPINGS (O4). | documented in the recipe `why` |
| `ui-contract/src/compare.ts:97-115` `starColour` | A hand-set colour ramp by Teff (7 stops, 2500–30000 K) for star discs on the Sizes tab. | It is quick, and "presentation only". | Use `thermalColour(teff)` from light.ts (the same CIE chain as the Eye recipe), so a star has one colour everywhere. Today the Sizes tab and the Eye view can disagree. | documented as "rough"; **cheap to replace** |
| `reference-objects.json:23` brown dwarf | A "typical" 66000 km and a hand colour `#8a4b3a`. | No single real brown dwarf stands for all of them. Colour from a cool-atmosphere spectrum, not a blackbody (molecular absorption dominates). | `typical: true`; the radius claim is unverified |
| `reference-objects.json:32` neutron star | One pulsar (PSR J0030+0451, 12.71 km) stands for "typical", with a hand colour `#c9d6ff`. | It is a measured, citable value near the middle of the NICER range. | Keep the measured value, and say "one measured pulsar" in the type. The colour could come from `thermalColour` of its surface temperature (about 10⁶ K gives blue-white), which agrees. | `typical: true`; documented |
| `reference-objects.json` stellar Teffs | Teff values rounded (Arcturus 4290, Sirius B 25000) or from another work than the one cited (Sirius A, α Cen B, Proxima). | Written from memory. | Use the cited value or cite the Teff separately (see fixes). | to fix |
| `compare.ts:11-13` | "× Earth" and "× Jupiter" use mean radii (6371.0, 69911), while IAU nominal radii are equatorial (6378.1, 71492). | They match the fact-sheet radii of the objects. | No change of physics; state "mean radius" in the size words or tooltip. | undocumented in UI |
| V14 measure (`handover/packages/V14.md:19`) | Distances between bodies assume circular, coplanar orbits at the ruler's time. | The chart draws orbits that way. | Kernel ephemeris positions (Kepler elements with e, i, Ω, ω) at the ruler's epoch. | documented in the panel text |
| `ui-contract/src/sound.ts:85,200` | Kepler's third law in AU and M☉ is multiplied by the Julian year (365.25 d) rather than the Gaussian year (365.2569 d). The planet mass is left out of M. | It is simple, and the 7 ppm error is inaudible. | Use the kernel's orbital period fact (already derived by the orbit flows), and fall back only when it is absent. | undocumented; trivial |
| `sound.ts:145-150` octave bands; `:109` scale | Pitch bands by mass and a just-intonation "settled" dial. | A designed sound, labelled so in the Why text and in the honesty label. | None (a mapping, not physics). It stays a documented design choice. | documented |
