| `sse-nav/src/sky.rs` `window` star brightness | The star's magnitude scales with bolometric luminosity, not its visual band | Needs a spectrum to integrate | V-band flux from the star's spectrum (O1/O12 `read_spectrum`) | stand-in until the light track's spectra are wired in |
| `packs/base-nav` `nav.window.sensors` | Three sensors with rough limiting magnitudes and resolutions | Sensors by band (O track) not built | Sensor arrays by band with apertures, exposures and noise | stand-in until O-track sensors |
| `sse-kernel` `sift` rarity | Hits carry no rarity (no resolve clouds passed) | Not wired yet | Rarity from the parent's resolve cloud (`criterion_probability`) | stand-in |
| The lander on the ground (LR6, LR6b), `ui-contract/src/landing.ts` `landerHeightOf`, `lampRadiance`, `LAMP_SPILL`; `renderer/src/surface-lander.ts`; Land's `HULL_GREY`, `LAMP_K` | A stock lander is a stock shape, its height that of a cube of its mass at 50 kg/m³; the party's ship is its own designed model (LR6b) standing on its tail, drive down, with no legs drawn and its radiators stowed (not drawn) while the drive is off; on either, the floodlamps are discs of a twenty-fifth of that mass-cube height putting out 500 W each, warm white (4000 K); their light back on the hull is one spill at a quarter of its height, falling with the lamps' size over the hull's squared on a longer ship; with the lamps lit the eye adapts so the hull reads mid grey; the lamps light no ground | A ship's design has no landing legs or stowed-radiator pose, and no record of which way up it lands (tail-sitter or belly lander); the surface's ground shading takes no point lights | Landing gear and stowed radiators in the ship design, and a "lands on" choice (tail or belly); lamp fit from its pack; each lamp's cone lighting the ground and the hull in the surface shader; exposure from the scene's mean luminance | picture choice, LR6, LR6b |
| The ship in orbit on the table (LR4c), `views/body/src/PlayerOrbit.svelte` | The party's ship is a named marker going round the players' orbit globe once every 24 s on a ring tilted to about a quarter, drawn clear of the limb | The seat has no orbit for the ship (Voyages records arrival, not an orbit), and a real low orbit would hug the limb and take hours | The ship's own orbit from its flight log (height, inclination, period), drawn to scale on the globe | presentation drift, LR4c |

| GZ-F12. Galaxies nearby's streams and Play (GZ6), `ui-contract/src/galaxies-nearby.ts` `streamArc`, `STREAM_ARC_GYR` 1.5, `pathAt` | A torn-apart satellite's stream is drawn as one faint arc along its own kernel path, from half a Gyr before it was torn apart (or the last 1.5 Gyr, whichever is later) to where it has got to; a shell or mixed remains is drawn the same way. Play moves each satellite straight between the kernel's path points, 0.1 Gyr apart (the orbit's own 0.02 Gyr points, one in five) | The kernel's remains (GH4, F49) are densities placed along the same path; drawing each as its density would need the remains read for every member at every frame | Draw GH4's remains themselves (blobs along the path with their spread, shells at the turning point, the mixed cloud), read once per member | stand-in |
| GZ-F13. The group's edge (GZ6), `ui-contract/src/galaxies-nearby.ts` `zeroVelocityRadius`, `groupEdge` | The edge of our group is Sandage's zero-velocity radius R0³ = 8GMt²/π² of the mass inside it (home's halo plus every catalogued halo inside, widened until it settles), with no dark energy, the group taken as a sphere around home and its mass as the haloes' r200 masses | The timing argument for a pressureless sphere is the classic estimate and needs only a mass and an age | The turnaround radius with a cosmological constant (which widens it by some per cent), from the kernel's own cosmology, the group's mass from its members' orbits | stand-in |
| GZ-F14. Galaxies nearby in True (GZ5 part 2), `renderer/src/galaxy-types.ts` `GALAXY_TYPES`, `TYPE_DECADES` 2.2; `views/cluster-map/src/Nearby.svelte` `lightOf` | A galaxy whose chart is not read is drawn as a type picture by rule: the kernel's type picks a bulge share (elliptical all, lenticular half, barred a fifth, disc 0.15, irregular and dwarf none), bulge size, two log-spiral arms (pitch 20 or 18 degrees, contrast 0.7), a bar of 0.6 R_e, lumps from value noise; young knots in blue by the True galaxy's young-light share; a log stretch 2.2 decades deep; the picture fades over its outer quarter. Shapes are as seen from home (the sky's tilt and turn). On the page each galaxy's brightness is 0.55 to 1 by its absolute V magnitude, and our own galaxy is its real face from the galaxy chart laid flat in our disc's plane | A charted face for every galaxy in view would cost a full chart read each; the type is what the kernel already says of each galaxy | Each galaxy's own chart face (galaxy-true.ts) read in the background for the big ones (GZ6 part 3), bulge-to-total and arm pitch from the galaxy's own state, brightness by apparent surface brightness and an exposure | stand-in |
## Candidates to move into flows

Logic written in Rust that could be an editable, cited flow of small blocks (Alex, 19 Nov: "try and do everything in
the physics chain blocks... other than Big Blocks. And have a UI element that makes sense to visualise").

| Where | What it computes | Why it is not a flow yet | Blocks it would need | View that would show it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ui-contract/src/galaxies-nearby.ts` `zeroVelocityRadius` (GZ6) | The zero-velocity radius of a mass at a time (Sandage 1986) | A one-line timing argument written beside the view that draws it | mass, age, G; a cube root | Galaxies nearby's edge of the group |

## Weather (track W, `crates/sse-weather`, `packs/base-weather`)

Details per package in `handover/packages/W1.md` to `W6.md`.

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| packs/base-weather `weather.circulation.wind_m_s` 12 m/s | One eddy wind scale for every world with a surface in the Rhines cell count | Rhines needs an eddy speed; tuned so Earth gets 3 cells | Eddy speed from each world's baroclinic energy budget | tuned, editable |
| `weather.circulation.delta_h` 1/3, `tropopause_scale_heights` 1.8 | Fixed equator-pole contrast and tropopause depth in Held and Hou | Both needed; Earth values | Radiative-convective column per world | tuned |
| `weather.supply.*` | Rain = 0.5 of sunlight reaching wet ground as latent heat; ground sunlight 0.67 (column/Earth)^-0.75; cloud 0.75 (1 − e^(−latent/35 W m⁻²)) | Fitted to Earth, Venus and Titan | Radiative transfer and a moisture budget | tuned |
| `weather.circulation` rain profile weights, rain belt following 0.35 to 0.6 of the sun | Shapes of wet and dry belts | Earth-like look | Zonal-mean energy balance (W6's block is a start) | tuned |
| `weather.photochemical` deck optical depth 30 (Venus) | Stand-in depth | No chemistry model yet | Photochemistry block | stand-in |
| `weather.photochemical` organic haze optical depth 9.6 and radius 0.72 µm (W10a) | Titan's measured haze (Huygens DISR fits summed at 500 nm; aggregates' equal-volume radius) used for every methane world under sunlight | One world measured; the fits were read from the DISR results slides, the 2008 paper itself not yet | Haze production from methane photolysis and the star's ultraviolet, settling by particle size (Lavvas et al. 2010) | measured on Titan, carried to others |
| `weather_giants.clouds.chromophore` (W10a): 1 µm optical depth 0.06 over zones to 0.6 over belts, radius 0.15 to 0.29 µm, colour [0.55, 0.27, 0.13] | Jupiter's fitted chromophore (Sromovsky et al. 2017) on every giant whose top deck is ammonia ice, Saturn included; drawn by the zone weight; colour a stand-in | Saturn's own fits (Baines et al. 2019) not read; the data sheet's chromophore row (MP33) not on main yet | Chromophore from ammonia and acetylene photochemistry (Carlson et al. 2016) with the sheet's optical constants | measured on Jupiter, colour stand-in |
| `weather_giants.clouds.particle_radius_um` ammonia ice 1.0 µm; `weather_formations.dust.particle_radius_um` 1.5 µm (K-c) | One radius per condensate everywhere on the deck; Mars's dust size for every dust storm | Braude et al. 2020's fit and Lemmon et al. 2015's 1 to 2 µm; other condensates (water, methane, sulphuric acid) carry no size until their papers are read | Sizes from each cloud's microphysics (updraft, supersaturation, settling) | measured, carried |
| circulation.rs `p_sat` | Clausius-Clapeyron with one latent heat from the triple point | Simple, published constants (NIST) | Full vapour-pressure fits per range | documented |
| `weather_formations.t_contrast_k` 40 K × (column/Earth)^-½ | Latitude temperature profile | Needed for sea temperature and snow | Energy-balance model | stand-in |
| `weather_formations.cyclones.*` and Eady's 3.9 | Storm life 5 d, 25 m/s, winter +30 %, N 0.012 s⁻¹; 3.9 not yet read from Eady's paper | Earth-like | Baroclinic growth from each world's shear and stratification | to review |
| `weather_formations.tropical.*` (80 a year, warm fraction 0.1, life, size, tracks) | Counts and motion | Earth-like | Potential intensity and genesis indices (Emanuel) | stand-in |
| `weather_formations.dust.*`, `orography.rain_per_m_s` 20, lightning storm density, marine layer 0.35, visibility 50 km, locked day-night 45 K | Stand-in constants | No data yet | Dust lifting thresholds; linear upslope rain (Smith and Barstad); convective mass flux; lower-tropospheric stability | stand-in |
| `weather_giants.clouds.heights` (W-heights): dry adiabat with ideal-gas c_p (5/2, 7/2, 4 R), isothermal above 0.1 bar, deck half a scale height thick, fallback bases 5, 2 and 0.7 bar, a deck pushed half again deeper to keep the order | Deck bases where the vapour saturates on that adiabat; no latent heating on the way up, no ortho-para hydrogen, no reaction clouds (ammonium hydrosulphide takes the fallback) | Gives Jupiter's ammonia base 0.73 bar, Saturn's 1.49, Uranus's and Neptune's methane 1.28 and 1.42, near the measured cloud tops | Moist adiabat and an equilibrium cloud condensation model (Atreya and Wong 2005) with the NH₃ + H₂S reaction | stand-in, close |
| hooks.rs descent bands (0.24, 0.83 scale heights), also the weather field layers' `base_m` and `top_m` on worlds with a surface (W-heights), icing 248 to 273 K, hazard thresholds, thunder heard to 15 km | Column and hazard rules | Readable descent telemetry | A real column model and aviation hazard rules | stand-in |
| `weather_giants.stratification.brunt_s` 0.0148 s⁻¹ | One N for every giant, fitted so Saturn's polar Bu = 1.6e-3 | L_d needs a stratification | Radiative-convective column per giant | tuned |
| `weather_giants.polygon.wavelength_ld` 5.07, side window 3 to 8, jet at 70° or more and 100 m/s or more | Polygon rule fitted to Saturn's 6 sides | Stated rule | Jet linear stability (Morales-Juberías 2015) | tuned |
| `weather_giants.bands.eddy_gas_m_s` 17, `eddy_ice_m_s` 100 (and the same pair in `base-surface regime_features.clouds`) | One eddy speed per giant kind, the same at every latitude, in the Rhines jet count round(½ sqrt(aΩ/U)) and the `jets` profile | Rhines needs the local eddy speed, not the jet speed (visual polish citation check, section 6: 10 to 40 m/s on Jupiter). 17 sits inside that and gives Jupiter 13 prograde jets (6 to 7 a hemisphere observed) and Saturn 12; no eddy speed is measured on the ice giants, so 100 is held to keep Uranus and Neptune at their 3 jets | Eddy speed by latitude from each giant's heat flux and stratification (Gastine et al. 2014 scalings) | tuned (K-a) |
| giants.rs circumpolar jet at (U a²/2Ω)^⅓ from the pole | Own derivation (polar γ-plane Rhines), with the jet speed scale (150 m/s), not the eddy speed | Puts Saturn's jet at 78.6° with no tuning; the eddy speed would move it to about 84° | Zonal-mean or shallow-water jets | to review |
| `weather_giants.polar.*` diffuse-cap Bu 4e-3, internal 0.1 W/m², transition drawn 50/50, ring and vortex sizes; band jet strengths; storm family rates | Regime thresholds and storm statistics | Between published values; tuned to the record | Shallow-water parameter sweep (a later simulation block) | tuned |
| `weather_aerosols` plume scaling to other worlds by gravity, density and stratification; buoyant to ballistic at 1e-4 bar | Mastin's Earth constant carried to other worlds | Only Earth calibrations exist | Buoyant plume model per atmosphere (Morton, Taylor and Turner with entrainment) | stand-in |

| `weather_aerosols.fire.min_o2` 0.15; impact thresholds and cooling; smog, spray and sulphur haze constants | Stand-ins, no number cited | No source read yet | Combustion limits (literature), impact-winter models, emission inventories | stand-in |

### The one climate (W7a, `crates/sse-climate`, `packs/base-climate`)

Moved here from the rows above: the 12 m/s Rhines wind, Held and Hou's contrast and tropopause, and `weather.supply` now
live under `climate.circulation` and `climate.supply`, with the same values; `weather_formations.t_contrast_k` and the
surface's own `cover.contrast_k`, `wear.rain_m_yr` and `wear.lapse_k_m` are gone (cover, wear and weather read the
energy balance). Details in `handover/packages/W7a.md`.

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `climate.heat_capacity.ground_inertia_si` 1500 | One ground thermal inertia for every world's land | Cover does not yet publish rock, regolith and ice per cell | Inertia per cell from S7 cover and S9 strata | stand-in until W7c |
| `climate.heat_capacity.mixed_layer_m` 50 | One ocean mixed layer depth everywhere | The standard EBM value (North and Coakley 1979) | Mixed layer from wind and stratification | documented |
| `climate.moisture.hadley_width_x` 0.3, scaled by sin(Hadley edge)/sin 30° | Siler et al. fit σ = 0.3 to Earth; the scaling to other worlds is ours | The paper is Earth only; a world's Hadley cell width is the natural stretch | A Hadley cell moisture budget from W7b's circulation | our extension, to review in W7b |
| `climate.moisture.h_top_ratio` 1.06 | The cell top's moist static energy as 1.06 × the equator's | Siler et al.'s Earth value | Gross moist stability from the column | tuned (Earth) |
| `climate.moisture.exchange_m_s` 0.0115 | One bulk exchange C_E·|U| for evaporation | Fitted to Earth's mean rain (about 1 m a year) | Surface wind from W7b times a drag law | tuned |
| `climate.defaults` spin 10 h, tilt 20°, default air | A body with no published spin, tilt or air gets these, and the climate's `assumed` list says so | W7b: formation runs (F4's settled spin, Kokubo and Genda 2010) and V3 imports (marked "authored (V3)") now hand spin and tilt to the `Body`; the defaults remain for Classic runs and bodies with no value | Tidal despinning over the world's life (formation's spins are primordial) | stand-in for missing values |
| `wear.wet_floor_k` 275 K | A world wet only in the past (early Mars) rains at no less than 275 K | The atmosphere history publishes no past temperatures | Past climate from the volatile history (W8 and S17) | stand-in until W8 |
| sse-surface `climate::conditions` flux 1361 / a² | Sunlight from a sun-like star when a body gives no flux | Surface bodies carry the star's climate fit but not its luminosity at the epoch | Flux from the star's luminosity at the epoch | stand-in |
| locked worlds | Treated latitudinally (one zonal climate), not day and night | The EBM is zonal | Day-night two-box or 2D EBM (W7b) | stand-in until W7b |
| moons | A moon's year is its orbit around the planet, so its seasons follow that orbit | `Body.orbit` is the moon's own | The planet's orbit around the star for insolation | known limit, to fix in W7c |

### Circulation strengths (W7b, `crates/sse-climate/src/strengths.rs`, `climate.strengths`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `climate.strengths.eddy_share` 0.4 | The Rhines eddy wind is 0.4 × the thermal wind scale | Replaces W7a's one 12 m/s with a speed that follows each world's forcing; 0.4 keeps Earth's three cells | Eddy kinetic energy from the baroclinic energy budget | tuned (Earth), editable |
| strengths.rs wind cap sqrt(R T Δ_H) | Where Ro_T ≫ 1 the thermal wind R T Δ_H/(2Ωa) is capped at the speed the contrast drives with no spin | The thermal wind diverges as Ω → 0 | Cyclostrophic balance and angular momentum budget of the slow rotator | our construction |
| `super_rotation_critical` 1, `super_rotation_steepness` 2 | Strength x²/(1+x²), x = Ro_T | Mitchell and Vallis give the onset near Ro_T ≈ 1 (1.3 in their transition run) but no strength curve | Fit to a GCM sweep of Ro_T | onset cited, curve ours |
| `stable_share` 0.35 | N² = 0.35 (g/T)(g/c_p) for every troposphere | Gives Earth's N ≈ 0.011 s⁻¹ | N from a radiative-convective column | tuned (Earth) |
| `lindzen_farrell` 0.31 | The factor in σ = 0.31 f dU/dz / N | Confirmed only by a citing source (NCAR NCL); the paper is a scan | Read the paper | unverified |
| `drag_coefficient` 0.001, `log_pressure_span` 1 | Komacek and Showman's hot-Jupiter timescale ratio used on rocky worlds, drag from Koll and Abbot's C_D a / H, Δln p = 1 | The only closed form for day-night contrast across both | Koll and Abbot 2016's rocky-planet theory (optically thin and thick nights) | to review; it gives Earth-air locked worlds very small contrasts and thin CO₂ air softer contrasts than Wordsworth 2015's GCM |
| night-side temperature T⁴(1 − A), floor at Wordsworth's thin radiator | A simple blend of full redistribution and none | Needs only A; the floor is the paper's own lower limit | Two-column day-night energy balance (W7c) | our construction |
| `collapse_width` 0.05 | Collapse turns on over ±5 % of the frost point | A smooth step instead of a gate | Night-side frost deposition rate against transport (exchange block, W8) | ours |
| `molecule_diameter_m` 3.7e-10 | One molecule size for every air | N₂'s kinetic diameter; Kn spans many decades so the size barely matters | Per-gas diameters | documented |
| label rules: airless when collisional < 0.5, thin-air when the radiative time is under 10 days, slow-rotator when super-rotation > 0.5 | Labels read off strengths | Labels only: the layout and winds never read them (weather formations still do, until W10) | None needed for labels | permanent for labels |
| sse-surface `climate::conditions` perihelion at 0° | A body's perihelion falls at its northern spring equinox | `Body.orbit` has no longitude of perihelion | Read it from the orbit (formation and imports), then precess it (W9) | stand-in |
| ebm.rs rising branch at the season's moist-static-energy peak, placed by a parabola through the top three bands | The Hadley share's centre follows the energy-flux equator | Siler et al. centre it on the equator for Earth's annual mean; a seasonal, tilted world needs it to move | Energy-flux-equator theory with the cross-equatorial cell (Bischoff and Schneider 2014) | our extension |
| cell layout (Hadley at least 8°, 8° per poleward cell, rounding) | Integer cells from the Rhines count | A layout needs whole cells | Jets from the zonal momentum budget | registered (review section 3) |

### Water supply and saltation (W9, `crates/sse-climate/src/water.rs`, `saltation.rs`, `climate.water`, `climate.saltation`, `climate.areas`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `water.sigma_k` 5 K | One scatter of air temperature about a season's mean, for the rain-snow split and the degree days, on every world | Calov and Greve's Greenland example; Titan's real scatter is far smaller | Scatter from the climate's own diurnal and synoptic variance | tuned (Earth), editable |
| `water.snow_offset_k` 1.0 for every liquid | Jennings et al.'s mean water threshold (it moves 0.8 K per 10 % humidity) used as an offset above any liquid's freezing point | One number, cited for water | Humidity-dependent threshold; per-substance hydrometeor melting | water cited; other liquids ours |
| `water.ddf_snow_mm` 3.2, `ddf_ice_mm` 8.0 per Earth day | Degree-day melt on every world and for every ice | Braithwaite's Greenland energy-balance factors; no other world has any | Surface energy balance melt (sunlight, longwave, turbulent fluxes) per substance | Earth cited; elsewhere a stand-in |
| water.rs potential evaporation | Energy-limited evaporation: `supply.latent_share` of the sunlight reaching the ground, only while unfrozen | Uses the climate's own energy budget, no Thornthwaite fit | Penman-Monteith with the surface wind and humidity | ours (gives about 1.2 m a year in the subtropics) |
| water.rs annual budget | Snow, melt, evaporation and runoff are summed over the year, with no storage from season to season | One budget per epoch is what geology needs | Seasonal snowpack and soil storage | ours |
| water.rs areas (Köppen) | Kottek et al.'s criteria on band temperatures, with the summer half taken as the seasons warmer than the place's mean, and Af/Aw split by P/PE ≥ 1 rather than the driest month | Our seasons are band means, not calendar months | Monthly rain at the place | labels only |
| `saltation.impact_reference_kg_m3` 1.2 | The impact threshold is 0.82 × the fluid threshold evaluated with air no thinner than Earth's | Kok 2010 finds Mars's impact threshold about Earth's, an order below its fluid threshold; this keeps that. Pluto's comes out near 3 m/s at 10 m, against Telfer et al.'s 8.5 to 10 m/s | Kok's numerical saltation model | ours (cited direction) |
| `saltation.gusts_per_year` 52,596, one start a year | Sand moves whenever the wind is above the impact threshold, if at least one ten-minute wind a year passes the fluid threshold | Kok's hysteresis says transport runs down to the impact threshold once started; how long a run lasts is not in our model | Event statistics of starts and stops | ours |
| `wind10.gust_share` 0.5 (`crates/sse-climate/src/wind.rs`) | The 10 m wind is the circulation's prevailing wind plus storms as an isotropic Gaussian vector wind (Monahan 2006); each component spreads by 0.5 × the eddy wind × the eddies' share of the heat transport (1 − the moisture block's Hadley share), slower as the cube root of the air's density over Earth's | The sea's waves read it. Blown sand still reads W9's `saltation.ten_metre_share` and `gust_share` (below): on this wind Mars's dunes would stop, because thin air's stronger gusts (the convective boundary layer) are not in the spread yet | The spread from the eddy kinetic energy budget; Monahan's skewed (non-Gaussian) components | fitted (Earth: a storm-track spread of about 5.7 m/s; mean speeds 8.3 m/s at 50°, 6 m/s in the trades, against about 9 to 11 and 7 measured) |
| `saltation.ten_metre_share` 0.5, `gust_share` 0.04, cube root of air density | The 10 m wind as half the climate's near-ground band wind, plus gusts from the eddy wind that slow in dense air | Earth's band winds of about 12 m/s to the observed 6 to 7 m/s over the sea; Venus and Titan near 1 m/s at the ground | A boundary-layer model with the wind's own distribution | tuned |
| two 10 m winds | Blown sand (W9) and sea waves read different 10 m winds | See the two rows above | One wind with gusts from the boundary layer's own turbulence, thin air included (W10) | known gap |
| 10 m wind in the deep tropics | The Hadley share leaves almost no spread at the equator, so the doldrums read 1 to 3 m/s against about 4 to 5 measured | Convective gusts and easterly waves are not modelled | Tropical transients from the convection (W10) | known gap |
| saltation.rs Weibull k = 2 everywhere | One gust distribution for every world | Kok 2010's choice; k is 1.5 to 3 on Earth | k from the climate's variance | cited, editable |
| `saltation.grains` 250 µm, methane ice below 90.67 K, water ice below 180 K, else rock (2650 kg/m³) | The sand's size and material from the ground's temperature | Geology does not yet publish its loose grains | Sand supply and composition from S9 cover and strata | stand-in |
| `saltation.lofting_width` 0.03 | Lofting where the coldest season is below the main gas's frost point, over ±3 % | Telfer et al.'s mechanism needs subliming ice; our climate does not freeze its air out yet, so Pluto's lofting reads about 0.01 | W8 `sse.volatile.exchange` and S17 ground ice | stand-in until W8 |
| `saltation.mobility.aridity_floor` 0.01 | Lancaster's M = W / (P/PE) divides by at least 0.01, so a rainless world needs W ≥ 0.5 % | Lancaster's index is infinite without rain | None: a floor keeps the index finite | ours |
| no sand supply | Dunes wherever the wind and dryness allow | Venus's dune fields are few (Magellan) because its sand is scarce | Sand budget from geology (S9) | to add |
| Titan's dunes | Our Titan mean wind near 0.5 m/s never reaches its 2 m/s threshold, so no dunes yet | Titan's dunes are built by rare strong equinox winds | W10 storms as strengths | known gap, W10 |

### Volatile species (W8 part 1 and matter pack MP3: `sse.volatile.species`, `crates/sse-climate/src/settings.rs`, `ebm::p_sat`, `packs/base-reference`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sse_reference::sublimation_pa` below a fit's range | Clausius-Clapeyron with the slope at the lowest fit's lower edge | Fray and Schmitt's polynomials run away outside their data (CO2's A5/T⁵ overflows near 1 K) | Measured curves to lower temperatures, or the third-law extrapolation Fray and Schmitt describe | documented |
| `ebm::p_sat` above the triple point | Clausius-Clapeyron over the liquid from the triple point with one heat of vaporisation | As before W8; Earth's warm bands keep their numbers | Liquid vapour curves on the sheet (NIST's Antoine fits) | documented |
| sheet `density_solid` of N2 (1027), CO (929), Ar (1616) and CH4 (494) kg/m³ | Values from memory, marked unverified on the sheet | Needed only to turn a mass of ice into a depth | Read from a measurement compilation (MP13) | stand-in |
| sheet condensed colours of CH4, NH3, N2, CO and Ar | Near-white authored colours (the climate pack's, moved) | No measured cloud colours yet | Optical constants per ice (MP13) | authored |
| one latent heat per cloud substance | `latent_j_kg` is the heat of vaporisation where the sheet has one, else of sublimation, else the curve's slope at the triple point | The cloud's latent flux uses one number; frost points read the curve itself | Phase-resolved latent heat (liquid or solid by the band's temperature) | documented |

### Moving ices (W8 part 2: `sse.volatile.exchange`, `crates/sse-climate/src/exchange.rs`, `climate.exchange`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `exchange.frost_emissivity` 0.9 | One thermal emissivity for every frost, times the world's own greenhouse (its outgoing flux at the mean temperature over σT̄⁴) | The climate's outgoing-longwave line A + B T, fitted at the mean, overstates what a 148 K polar night radiates (about twice σT⁴ on Mars), which doubled Mars's caps | Per-ice emissivity on the sheet (CO2 frost 0.8 to 0.95, N2 ice 0.5 to 0.8, Lewis et al. 2020) and a radiative column | ours: 0.9 taken from the observed range, after which Mars came out at 29 % (61 % with the linear line) |
| frosted bands' sunlight | A frosted band absorbs with the climate's albedo, not its ice's | No per-ice albedo yet; Pluto's 0.72 is its whole-world value | Ice albedo per species and grain size (S19) | documented |
| one gas | Only the air's main gas exchanges; minor ices (Mars's water, Pluto's methane and CO) do not | The main gas sets the pressure; the others ride on it | One exchange per species, each with its partial pressure | known gap |
| no ground ice given | The air is the inventory, and each spin-up year restores the given mean pressure | The world's given pressure is its observed mean; without it Mars's perennial cap would draw the air down without limit | Ground inventories from formation and outgassing (S17, F stages) | documented |
| migration (`migration_steps` 40, `migration_span_yr` 1 Myr) | A year's change per band is carried forward until the first band that is losing ice would run out, then the ground's total is restored | Hundreds of orbits of stepping cost too much at the Orbit level; the equilibrium is what the surface reads | Stepping through the obliquity cycle (W9's spin history), with flow and basins (S17) | documented |
| isothermal ice at the climate's temperature | A buffered world's ice sits at the frost point the climate's mean temperature gives: Pluto's fixture at 40 K holds about 12 Pa, where the real ice at 37 K holds about 1 Pa | The bright ice is colder than the darker bare ground; with one albedo the ice takes the mean | Albedo contrast between ice and bare ground | known gap |
| `Curve` table | The vapour curve is read from a 1,024-node table in 1/T per cycle | The exchange reads it thousands of times a year; ln P is near-linear in 1/T | None needed | documented |

### Land and sea columns (W7c part 1, `crates/sse-climate/src/ebm.rs` `cycle_two`, `climate.land_sea`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| two columns per band | Each band's land and sea are two well-mixed columns; a place's distance from the coast does not yet matter | The Orbit level is zonal; coasts and interiors need the D2 grid | North, Mengel and Short 1983's 2D energy balance on the cell grid (W7c part 2 and W11) | documented |
| `land_sea.coasts_per_band` 2 | One continent and one sea round each band when the world gives no coasts (fixtures, unsurveyed worlds) | Only surveyed worlds have a height map; since W7c part 2a they count their own (`land_strips`) | None needed for surveyed worlds | documented |
| land strips from pre-wear Orbit-level heights | The climate's land is read off the Orbit-level (guide) tectonic or sketch heights before wear, with sea level for the history's water, at every detail level | Wear needs the climate's rain, so the climate cannot wait for the worn map | Iterate climate and wear once | documented |
| diffusive coupling | Heat crosses a coast by the model's own D over the lesser of half a block and ℓ = R √(D / \|B + iωC\|), the seasonal reach on each side; one reach per column from the seasonal frequency, used for the annual mean too | Exact for one damped diffusion across a coast at the seasonal frequency; the annual-mean exchange is slightly over-coupled | The 2D model | ours, derived |
| zonal advection, `land_sea.column_wind_ratio` 0.5 | The air column carries its heat round the band with half the thermal wind at the thermal depth (a wind rising linearly with height), relaxing to the new ground over u τ, τ = C_air / (ρ c_p C\|U\| + B), across one windward coast in two | Diffusion alone left Earth's land at 52° N swinging 57 K against about 30 to 35 K observed; with advection it gives 32 K and the sea 10 K (observed 8 to 10) | Winds per cell on the D2 grid | ours: the ½ is the linear-profile column mean, not fitted; the outcome was checked against the observed ranges, not tuned |
| `land_sea.max_coupling_w_m2_k` 50 | Coupling held below 50 W m⁻² K⁻¹ per square metre of band | The geometry grows as 1 / cos² φ at the poles | None needed (the cap only binds in the polar bands, where land and sea are already one) | documented |
| tied columns | A band with no land reports its sea for both columns (and one with no sea its land) | Keeps the solve one shape | None needed | documented |
| evaporation from the sea column | On a world with land and sea columns, the band's evaporation is the sea column's: q_sat at the sea's own temperature, into the air over it at the model's relative humidity, times the band's sea share | The sea warms and cools slower than the band's mean | Evaporation per cell from the sea surface temperature on the D2 grid | done (W7c part 2c); Earth's global mean 0.84 m a year, against about 1.0 measured |
| exchange on one column | W8's moving-ices cycle keeps the single mixed column | Frost of the main gas comes within reach only on worlds without a sea so far | Two columns in the exchange cycle if a world needs both | known gap |
| ice-age onsets | Dated only inside the W9 orbit-history window | Outside it there is no spin history to date them by | Longer secular tracks (T1) | documented |

### Currents (W7c part 2b, `crates/sse-weather/src/formations.rs`, `weather_formations.currents`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `currents.upwelling_km` 500, `upwelling_cooling_k` 5 | Under the subtropical high, a sea place within reach of a coast lying to the spin's east is cooled by 5 K × exp(−d / 500 km) and carries the marine deck; the open ocean and the far shore carry none | The deck needs cold water (Klein and Hartmann 1993) and the cold water is eastern-boundary upwelling; read along the parallel, 16 samples out to 2000 km | Ekman transport from the coast-parallel wind, and an ocean model with gyres | read off observation (Californian, Peruvian, Canarian, Benguela coasts) |
| `currents.boundary_km` 300, `boundary_warming_k` 4, `gyre_half_width_deg` 15 | Within 15° of the Hadley edge, a sea place near a coast lying to the spin's west is warmed by 4 K × exp(−d / 300 km) | Western intensification of the gyres (Stommel 1948) puts warm currents on those coasts | As above | read off observation (Gulf Stream, Kuroshio) |
| fixtures without a map | A world with no height map keeps the marine deck over every subtropical sea, at its ocean share | No coasts to read | None needed once every world has a map | documented |

### Orbital forcing and ice ages (W9 part 2, `crates/sse-climate/src/forcing.rs`, `climate.forcing`, `crates/sse-kernel/src/ice_ages.rs`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `forcing.ellipticity_spin_power` 2 | Dynamical ellipticity E_d = Earth's 0.00327381 × (ω/ω_Earth)² | A hydrostatic bulge grows as the spin squared; Mars's real E_d is larger (Tharsis), so non-hydrostatic worlds are off | E_d from the interior model and loads (S2) | ours |
| `forcing.moon_geometry` 1 | g_L = 1 in Zeebe's β | Reproduces Earth's 50.4″ a year with the Moon (test) | g_L from the moon's orbit inclination and eccentricity | tuned (Earth) |
| kernel `ice_ages.rs` drawn angles | Siblings' inclinations (up to 3°), nodes and perihelia drawn from each planet's address | Formation does not publish them; a coplanar draw would give no tilt cycles at all | Inclinations and angles from the formation run (F4) | stand-in, labelled in the track's source |
| Laplace-Lagrange (T1) | Secular theory only: no resonances, no chaos (Mars's tilt swings far wider in reality), no spin-orbit resonance capture | T1's quiet-stretch theory | Symplectic windows with the spin equations (T1's n-body) | documented |
| forcing.rs linear ice response | The ice balance at a place moves linearly with its summer energy, measured from one climate run at 1° less tilt | One extra climate per world instead of one per sample | Ice-sheet mass balance with ice-albedo and elevation feedback (ice sheets integrate, so real ice ages beat at 41 and 100 kyr) | ours |
| `forcing.window_yr` 400 kyr, `sample_yr` 2 kyr | One window read for the whole epoch | The review: ice ages are an average over the epoch's insolation, not stepped | The window chosen at the epoch's own time in T1's history | ours |
| advances counted as upward zero crossings | Each crossing of the ice balance from loss to gain is one advance | No hysteresis or minimum ice volume | Ice volume integrated through the window | ours |
| moons and lone worlds | A moon, or a planet with no siblings, gets no orbit track and so no cycles | A lone planet has no secular forcing; a moon's own forcing (its planet's wobble) is not modelled | Satellite secular theory | documented |

### Tides and wind waves (W-sea, `sse.tides.ocean_range`, `sse.tides.period`, `sse.ocean.wind_waves`, `crates/sse-kernel/src/sea.rs`, `climate.sea`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sse.tides.ocean_range` | The open-sea equilibrium tide, H = 3/2 γ (m/M)(R/d)³ R, with γ 1 (a fluid world on a rigid floor). No ocean dynamics: no basin resonance, no amphidromes, no shelf amplification | The card's figure is the tide the bodies raise; real coasts reach several to tens of times it (Fundy about 16 m) | Basin and shelf response from the height map (Laplace tidal equations per basin) | documented; the note on the card says so |
| tide raisers | The body the world orbits and its moons. A moon world's tide from its planet is shown, but not the star's; the world's own siblings are left out (each below a millionth of the Sun's on Earth) | The raisers that matter on every case met so far | All bodies within a set share of the largest | documented |
| spring and neap | Spring is the sum of the moving tides, neap the largest less the rest; a fixed bulge (a raiser the world keeps one face to) is shown but raises no tide | The extremes of tides adding in and out of phase; the timing of springs is not computed | Tides as harmonic constituents in the universe clock | documented |
| `sse.ocean.wind_waves` on other liquids (W-sea3: `climate.sea.reference_density_ratio` 0.0012) | The CEM's Earth-fitted growth reads the wind scaled by sqrt((ρ_air/ρ_liquid) / 0.0012), the card's `drive`: the waves' momentum comes from the air, their weight from the liquid. Viscosity is reported but does not damp the drawn trains; surface tension enters the trains' dispersion and the ripple cut-off | No published fitted growth law for other liquids has been read; the density ratio is the leading term | A growth law with the density ratio, viscous damping 2νk² and a capillary cut-off, checked against Titan's observed calm seas (Lorenz and Hayes; Grima et al.) | stand-in: Titan's methane gets a drive near 3, so its waves may be too high against the glassy seas seen |
| sea trains (W-sea3) | Five trains sampled from the Pierson-Moskowitz spectrum at ω_p × 1, 1.4, 2, 2.8, 4, fanned ±15° and ±30° about the wind, each from ω² = gk + (σ/ρ)k³ | A few numbers per region for a cheap shader | A directional spectrum (JONSWAP with a spreading function) sampled by the renderer | drawing stand-in |
| `climate.sea.whitecaps` (W-sea4) | Stramska and Petelski's developed-sea fit W = 5e-5 (U − 4.47)³ for any liquid, read with the driven wind; foam reflectance 0.22 flat (Koepke) | Only Earth's sea measured; foam on methane or helium follows its own bubble physics | Whitecaps from wave breaking (dissipation) and the liquid's foam stability | stand-in for other liquids |
| sea colour (W-sea2) | Deep colour R = 0.33 b_b/(a + b_b) in three bands from pure water's absorption and molecular scattering on the sheet: no plankton, sediment or salt (seawater scatters about 30% more), no sky reflection (the renderer adds it) | Pure liquid first; the sheet holds pure water only | Chlorophyll and sediment as dissolved and suspended matter (Morel and Maritorena 2001) | documented; other liquids say the sheet lacks their absorption |
| wind sea only | The waves are those the local wind grows; no swell runs in from distant storms | Swell needs a propagation model | Swell from the W2 storm tracks, decaying with distance | known gap: the calm tropics show about 0.1 to 0.7 m where altimetry shows about 1.5 to 2 m of swell |
| `sea.storm_fetch_m` 1000 km | The fetch in a band is its open sea between coasts, held to a storm's size | A wind system is only so large; the CEM's growth also stops at full development | Fetch along the wind from the cell grid and the storm's own extent | documented |
| card's wind | Each band's waves are the year's mean over its 10 m wind distribution (`crate::wind`), storms and calms alike | The mean of the waves each wind raises, not the waves of the mean wind | Waves per cell under the weather of the day | Earth's card: 2.4 to 2.7 m in the mid-latitudes, 1.6 m over all the sea (wind sea only; measured totals of about 2.5 to 3 m include swell) |
| frozen seas | A band's waves are worked out even where its sea is frozen over | The card has no sea-ice share per band yet | Sea ice from the climate's ice line | known gap |

## Future tech (`packs/base-tech`, track FT)

Rules of thumb behind the tech ladder (design section 15.7). Each rests on one or two cited examples, so each is an editable guess with history behind it, not a measurement. The examples themselves are cited in the design note's section 14.1.

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `families[engine].maturity` worse_by 7 | One level short, an engine's or power plant's output for its mass is a seventh | V-2 about 22 against Merlin 1D over 150; Jumo 004 1.2 against EJ200 about 9 (small sample) | A history of each part type's figure by year, fitted per family | Rule of thumb, small sample |
| `families[material].maturity` worse_by 5 | One level short, a bulk material's strength for weight is a fifth | carbon fibre 0.5 to 1 GPa (early 1960s), 3.5 (1971), 7.0 (now); nanotube tubes 100 GPa against fibre 4.2 (small sample) | As above | Rule of thumb, small sample |
| `families[store].maturity` worse_by 3 | One level short, an energy store holds a third | lithium-ion 80 Wh/kg in 1991, about three times that now (Crabtree 2015) (one example) | As above | Rule of thumb, small sample |
| `families[near-ceiling].maturity` toward 2/3 | One level short, a figure held down by a nearby ceiling is two-thirds of the way from the old figure to the mature one | V-2 exhaust about half the best hydrogen engine's (one example) | As above | Rule of thumb, small sample |
| `families[fictional].maturity` size 10, 0.5, 2 | Fictional and unknown devices: ten times the mass and volume per level short, half per level ahead for two levels | Alex's rule of thumb; nothing to go on | None: the device is fictional | Authored |
| `ladder.improve_per_level_beyond` 1.5 | Past the home level a real figure improves half as much again per level until its next cited mark | design 5.2 | As above | Rule of thumb |
| `figures[waste_heat_k]` 350 K and 300 K | Electronics and quarters reject heat at single round temperatures | design 15.2 | The ship's own thermal model per load | Authored |

## Game-system packs

Codes that a game reads from physics. Where a code's band had to be drawn on the physics, the line is a choice,
listed here. Imported codes always win over these (they are kept as authored and marked where physics disagrees).

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `scripts/build-traveller-pack.py`, rule `traveller.prep.oxygen` | Air "carries oxygen" when O2 is at least 10% of the surface pressure | Traveller's breathable bands are named, not defined by partial pressure | A breathing model per species (O2 partial pressure against altitude and acclimatisation), shared with the habitability atlas | permanent for the code; the habitability scheme (C2) carries the real need |
| `traveller.prep.tainted` | Tainted when CO2 > 0.005 bar, O2 > 0.5 bar, SO2 ≥ 1e-5 bar, or an authored `atmosphere.taint` | The published taints are a story list (pollutants, biologicals, radioactivity...) | Toxicity thresholds per gas from a gas table (V3's tags port in X3d gas mixes) | to review in X3d |
| `traveller.prep.corrosive`, atmosphere C | Corrosive when SO2 ≥ 1 mbar or the main gas is SO2, H2S, Cl2, F2, HCl or H2SO4; insidious is corrosive at 10 bar or more | No physical definition exists for "insidious" | Materials attack rates from composition, pressure and temperature | permanent for the code |
| Atmosphere E and F | Never produced by physics; authored with `atmosphere.traveller_kind` | "Low" and "unusual" describe places and times, not a mean state | Regional and time-varying air from the weather track (W) | stand-in until W |
| `traveller.count.gas-giant` (PBG G digit) | A gas giant is anything 2.5 Earth radii or larger and below 13 Jupiter masses | The games count ice giants and puffy sub-Neptunes alike | The physical atlas's giant classes once the one-body object settles mass and envelope | to review after the one-body unification |
| Trade codes Fr, Co, Ho, Tu, Tr | Mean surface temperature bands (below 223 K, 223 to 263 K, 303 to 353 K) | T5 places these by orbit against the habitable zone, which SSE4 does not keep as a fact | Climate classes from the weather track | to review with W |
| Size F, population F | Size F also holds anything over 23,200 km across; population F anything over 10^15 | The code has no symbol beyond F | None needed: the reading keeps the true value | permanent |
| Missing facts | Absent pressure, liquid cover, population, government, law and tech read as 0, starport as X | A profile always has every digit | None: each reading lists the facts that stood in (`defaulted`) | permanent |
| `crates/sse-codes/src/plan.rs`, `target_temperature`, `optical_depth` | A coded world is placed for 288 K if it has surface liquid (220 K under next to no air, 300 K if dry), through the grey-atmosphere atom with infrared optical depth 0.83 x pressure in bar, at albedo 0.3 | Codes carry no temperature; the orbit must come from somewhere | The climate flows' own greenhouse and albedo for the pinned air, solved for the orbit (C3 reverse placement through base-physics flows) | to review: wire to C3 placement |
| `plan.rs`, `EXOSPHERE_K`, `JEANS_KEEP` | Air retention runs the `sse.atmosphere.jeans_parameter` atom at the surface radius and a fixed 1000 K exosphere, keeping air when it is 30 or more | Exosphere temperature needs the upper-atmosphere energy balance, which no flow computes | Exosphere temperature from stellar XUV and composition; Jeans plus hydrodynamic escape | to review |
| `plan.rs`, `rocky_mass`, `star` | A coded world's mass is M = R^3.7 (Earth-like rock); stars other than class V are grown as dwarfs of the same spectral type, white dwarfs as 0.6 solar-mass dwarfs | Codes give size only; the formation pipeline grows stars from birth and no age is chosen yet to reach a giant or remnant | Composition from the system's formation run; an age solve that evolves the star to its written class | stand-in until the one-body star work |
| `plan.rs`, `giants_in` | A grown world counts as a gas giant from 10 Earth masses, whatever its envelope | The game counts ice giants and big sub-Neptunes alike | As the gas giant reading rule above | to review with it |
| `mix.rs`, `gas_mixes` | A coded world's air is a named preset: mid-range fractions scaled to one, at the pressure of the code's own class (not the preset's range) | No flow yet derives composition from outgassing and escape; the game names air by code | Per-gas partial pressures from an atmosphere flow (outgassing, escape, photochemistry) | to review |
| rules `traveller.need.*` | What to wear outside is read from the atmosphere digit, not from partial pressures and toxicity limits | The game's gear follows its codes | Physiological limits per gas (oxygen 0.16 to 0.5 bar, carbon dioxide and toxic-gas limits) as rules over partial pressures | to review |
| `scripts/build-traveller-pack.py`, `nav_ships.nav_setting.built_t_per_dt` (4 t per displacement ton) | A standard ship's dry mass is its hull tonnage times 4 t, cargo included | The game rates ships by volume (14 m³ a ton) and gives no masses | Mass from the ship designer's own parts (structure, quarters, hold, tanks, drive) once the designer reports a built mass | authored, to review with FT7 and FT8 |
| `nav_ships.nav_setting` drive (fusion torch, 3000 km/s, propellant 5% of dry mass) | The setting's thrust held for days is drawn and flown as a fusion torch, with its real waste heat and radiators | The game's in-system drive needs no reaction mass; skipping it is a future tech rule not yet built | The setting's skipped problems (reaction mass, waste heat) as marked fictions from the future tech packs (FT7, FT8) | stand-in until FT7 and FT8 |
| Traveller standard ships: cargo, crew and berths | Round values written for this pack | Hull tonnage, jump and thrust are the long-published ratings; the rest varies by edition and was not checked against a rulebook | The referee's own figures (edit the template or the ship), or a check against the edition a table plays | authored, unverified |

## Ship manifest and crew (track MC)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ui-contract/manifest.ts` `ageNow` | A person's own age moves on with campaign (home) time while awake or missing, and not at all in cold sleep | Voyages do not yet record ship time, and no hibernation metabolism is modelled | Ship time from each voyage leg (time dilation on fast flights), and an ageing rate in cold sleep from the setting's future tech | stand-in until voyages record ship time |
| `ui-contract/manifest.ts` `lifeSupport` | People in cold sleep draw nothing from the stores; anyone whose kind of being is not stated is counted at the human rates | Most campaigns start with human crews; the screen says so on the Life support line | Cold-sleep upkeep (power, a small share of oxygen) as a rate in the pack; no default kind once 4.1 aliens bring rates of their own | authored, to review with 4.1 life |
| `packs/base-manifest` `life_support.recycled` | One recovered share for oxygen and water together, set by the GM per ship | No life support plant is modelled; the share stands in for it | Each recovery system (water processor, oxygen generator, plant beds) as a part with its own efficiency and power draw, from the ship designer | authored |
| redox.rs `redox_iw` (F-redox, `sse.interior.redox_iw`) | The magma ocean's surface sits one fixed shift (2.7 log units, set so Earth lands at Sossi et al. 2020's IW+0.5) above its core-formation ΔIW for every world; bulk iron is Earth's 32% of rock and metal; FeO and iron mix ideally | Armstrong et al. 2019's self-oxidation grows with magma ocean depth (pressure); bulk iron follows the star's Fe/Mg/Si | Scale the shift with the magma ocean's base pressure; read bulk iron from MP6's composition | Stand-in (F-redox) |
| redox.rs `outgassed_mix` (F-redox, `sse.atmosphere.outgassed_mix`) | The outgassed H and C are split at one temperature (2000 K), methane in one step from the methane-free mix, under the weight of the gas counted as water and CO2 | Real magma oceans set their air over a cooling range; methane grows as the air cools and quenches | Equilibrate along the cooling path to a quench temperature (Gaillard et al. 2022) | Stand-in (F-redox) |

## Roll call (track SH, `ui-contract/rollcall.ts`, `shell/src/rollcall/`)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shell/src/rollcall/feed.ts` days of the "Sol today" pack | A day is read on the universe clock (TT), whose midnight runs about 34 s (1961) to 69 s (since 2017) ahead of UTC midnight, so a launch or landing in the last minute of a UTC day can show on the next day | The pack's times are TT seconds and the pack's calendar counts days on them; the screen says "about a minute from UTC" | A leap-second table (TAI minus UTC, with its source) as pack data, applied when a day is written | stand-in (SH2) |
| `ui-contract/rollcall.ts` `rollCall` placing a craft | A craft is placed at the clock's moment within the day; where its log has no piece then (the climb before its first orbit, a lander before it undocks) it is placed at the middle of its part of the day, then the day's end, then its start | Pack logs start at orbit insertion or undocking; the ascent and the docked stretch are not pieces yet | Ascent pieces (RS5) and docked pieces for landers, so every moment of a stay has a piece | stand-in (SH1) |
| `ui-contract/namescarried.ts` `orbitAt` (Names carried, SH7) | A named minor planet is placed by a two-body Kepler solve about the Sun from one set of osculating elements (JPL Horizons, at their epoch); the planets' pull is left out, so its place drifts from the real one the further the date is from that epoch | The layer and "tonight" need a place on any date with no ephemeris service; one element set per body is what the pack ships | Elements at several epochs (or Horizons vectors) as pack data, the nearest epoch used; or the n-body integrator with the planets | stand-in (SH7) |

## Power books (track RK, RK9)

| Where | Instead of the physics | Why | Real physics to replace it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `store-packs/paper-spacecraft` (RK6) figures marked `derived` | Where a study gives no figure the ship needs, the builder works one out from the study's own: HOPE's thrust from jet power and exhaust speed; Orion's thrust from the 0.3 g start-up and its dry mass from the rocket equation on the mission's 72,850 ft/s at 1,800 s; efficiencies from jet power over power in | A ship record needs thrust and both masses to fly; the derivation is the study's physics, not a tuned number | The figures from the parts of each study not yet read (HOPE's companion engine paper AIAA 2003-4526; GA-5009's vehicle tables) | stand-in (RK6) |
| `store-packs/paper-spacecraft` single-stage records (RK6 part 2) | Ships that drop parts on the way fly as one stage carrying everything: the DRA 5.0 ship keeps its two empty drop tanks after Earth departure; Longshot keeps its empty tanks and drive, so SSE4 says about 313 years to Alpha Centauri where the staged study says about 100 | NAV ships have no stages; the record is the study's start-of-mission mass | Stages on ship records (track RS split and join, RS4) read by NAV | stand-in (RK6) |
| `store-packs/paper-spacecraft` DRA 5.0 arrays | The 50 kWe arrays are a constant source at their Mars rating, not a sunlight law | The study rates them only at Mars; a sunlight law needs the flux they were rated at | A sunlight law with the reference flux read from the sheet at Mars's distance | stand-in (RK6) |
| `crates/sse-nav/src/insystem.rs` `belt_legs` for very low thrust (seen with RK6's fission fragment and nuclear electric ships) | A leg to or from a belt is offered as a Hohmann transfer without the RK5 short-kick check, so a 6e-5 g ship that stops at the Main Belt for fuel flies 460 days to it on burns that take months; the leg on to Jupiter is a straight-line torch flight. SSE4 says 1,064 days (HOPE NEP) and 1,629 (fission fragment) against the studies' 832 and over 2,100 | The body-to-body spiral (`courses.rs` spiral row) needs more Δv than these tanks hold after the reserve, so the search refuels at the belt, and belt legs predate RK5 | Belt legs spiral as body-to-body legs do (travel thread) | known gap (RK6 log, part 3) |
| `sse-kernel/src/nav.rs` `powered` | Sunlight power for a leg is held at the leg's dimmer end (the lower of the start and end worlds' starlight), not integrated along the path; between the stars no world's starlight is read, so sunlight power counts as none | A course's thrust must be one that the ship can keep the whole way; the cautious end is never generous | Thrust following the flux along the integrated course, with the array's pointing and temperature | stand-in (RK9 part 2) |
| frames.rs ice-age onsets (SF1 part 2, `ice_ages::orbit_inputs_from`) | The window before the epoch integrates the spin axis from today's tilt and perihelion at its start, not back from today | Spin history runs forward only; the tilt drifts about a degree in 400 kyr on Earth | Integrate the spin axis backwards from the epoch | Stand-in (SF1) |
| core.rs `CoreSettings.sigma_core` (MF2 part 1, `sse.dynamo.reynolds`) | Liquid iron's electrical conductivity is a typed 1e6 S/m for every core | The sheet has no liquid iron row yet (asked of the data sheet thread, 24 Nov); Pozzo et al. 2012 give about 1.5e6 | Read the sheet's `fe.liquid` sigma_elec through Look up (MF2 part 2) | Stand-in (MF2) |
| ledger.rs `flow_speed` (MF2, `sse.dynamo.flow_speed`) | Christensen and Aubert's flux Rayleigh number without its geometric factor (outer over inner radius), so a full sphere has an answer | Thin shells stir a little faster than this gives | Carry the factor when the inner core is large | Stand-in (MF2) |

| sse-atoms weathering.rs `TAU_1AU_S` (SW1) | Soil matures towards 1 exponentially, with one e-folding time at 1 AU (about 300 Myr) that folds in gardening | Fitted so the Moon's bright rays fade over about 1 Gyr (Grier et al. 2001); no per-process rates are wired yet | Separate solar-wind, micrometeorite and gardening rates (Pieters and Noble 2016), with the star's own wind (O10) | Stand-in (SW1) |

| `sse-gravity/src/bend.rs` bend table | Light paths for a mass that does not spin only; a spinning hole will be drawn with them (shadow size off by up to about 4%, the one-sided flattening missing) | Exact paths round a spinning hole need per-pixel integration; this table is exact for no spin and cheap on phones | The spin pass (XO7) on capable tiers; phones keep the table with the badge "bending drawn for no spin" | planned shortcut (design 9), XO1 |
| `sse-gravity/src/bend.rs` viewer far away | Rays are traced in from infinity; a camera within about fifty r_g reads the same curves from part-way along (XO4) | The far viewer covers every view outside the close-up | Start each ray at the camera's radius (same equation, one more number) | XO1; revisit in XO4 |
| `sse-gravity/src/bend.rs` table edges | Three half turns of sweep (direct image, arch, thin ring) and b up to 64 r_g; beyond, the weak bender's first term (within 6% at the edge, 0.1% with its next term) | Higher image orders are thinner than a pixel; far rays barely bend | None needed; add the second-order term to `sse.gravity.weak_bend` if a view ever shows the join | XO1 |
| `sse.gravity.shadow_angle` | Shadow size for no spin, static viewer | As the table | The spin pass's shadow (XO7) | XO1 |
| `chart-grammar/src/CompositeStack.svelte` (XO17) | Composite lays whole view layers in the browser: each band's picture tinted by its brightness (Rec. 709 weights), added with `plus-lighter` on a dark ground or `multiply` on the chart room's paper (the paper lifted to white by 1/0.88 first); each band arrives scaled to its own brightest by its own view | One mechanism for every view with no layering code of its own | Per-band render targets in one GPU context and `renderer/src/composite.ts` in a shader, with each band's true brightest | presentation, XO17 |
| `sse-hazard` belts away from the equator (XO18) | Along a field line the dose thins by `sse.belts.latitude_falloff`, Liouville on an untilted dipole for the belt model's own pitch-angle spread (A = 0.3, so it thins slowly); the loss cone is not cut. A dive "over the poles" is a parabola with its closest point over a pole, which crosses the equator's plane at twice that distance, inside the belts; with MF7's belts reaching Jupiter's cloud tops, a 10 g/cm² ship dives only to about 12 radii there, where Juno (a long ellipse, closest near the equator under the inner belt) passes at about 1.06 | The belt model does not carry pitch angles, tilt or the inner edge Juno found | A pitch-angle belt model (MF7), a tilted and offset field, and voyages' own courses through the same rows | planned shortcut, XO18 |
| `sse-hazard` shields outside the list | A shield lighter than the thinnest the belts list (3 kg/m²) reads as that one (low); heavier than the thickest (400 kg/m²) reads as that one (high); between two, ln dose straight across ln shield | The belts give six shields | More shields in `read_field`'s belt settings | XO18 |
| `sse-hazard` hull heat | The hull is taken as settled at the temperature the light sets (a quick dive reads a little hot); round hull f = 1/4, a face-on sunshade f = 1/2 (a thin plate giving heat off both faces) through `sse.climate.equilibrium_temperature` | No hull heat-capacity model yet | A hull warming over the pass from its heat capacity (sheet) | XO18 |
| `sse-hazard` clocks on a pass (XO20) | The pass is a Newtonian parabola; its clock runs at 1 − 2rg/r, the rate of a fall with the energy of rest far away; the parabola's own shape near a compact body (where it is no longer a parabola) is not followed | Fine for every body that is not compact (rg/r below 1e-5); a dive near a neutron star or black hole would need the bend table's paths | Geodesic passes from `sse-gravity` for compact bodies | planned shortcut, XO18 |
| `sse-hazard/data/hazard.json` tones | Harsh and deadly lines are policy: 0.05 Sv (ICRP's most in any one year) and 1 Sv (radiation sickness) for a day's stay or one pass; three quarters of the hull limit or of the fuel reads harsh | The GM needs a line; each is stated and editable | A pack or the GM sets their own; later a dose-to-effect block | presentation, XO18 |
| `sse-hazard` Dive | A fall from far away (a parabola) with its closest point on the chosen line; the air stops it at the top of the air (aerobraking is the landing blocks' job) | The quick answer; full courses are voyages' | A voyage's own course through the same rows | XO18 |

### Why the bend table is Rust, not a flow

It is a table of about 65,000 numbers worked out by stepping one differential equation two million times: an Equation block has no loops by design. It is the "heavy physics ships as tables behind swappable blocks" pattern: the table is the design's block `sse.gravity.light_path` (published to the screen with the op `read_light_paths` in XO2), worked out once, hashed (`tests/golden/gravity.json`) and checked the same bits in the browser harness. Every size it is read with (horizon, photon orbit, last stable orbit, shadow) is a formula block in the base pack.

Totals over many members match the cloud only on average, not exactly per unit. | A cloud carries one mean makeup; members must differ, and an exact split would mean resolving every member | Per-member makeup from the formation region it came from (a composition gradient across the belt, and families from known parents) | Stand-in; tuned, not fitted |
| SB-belt-gradient | sse-small, type labels | No S-inside, C-outside gradient within one belt: formation gives one makeup per cloud, so S and C types mix evenly across it (DeMeo and Carry 2014 see C rise outward) | Formation's clouds have one mean | Carry the disc's temperature gradient into each cloud (makeup by a) | To review with proven core |
| SB-differentiated | pack `makeup.differentiated_share` (asteroid belt 0.1, belt 0.05) and `layer_purity` 0.85 | A fixed share of primordial-belt members are fragments of a melted, layered parent (iron M, rocky V); the share is not computed from which planetesimals formed early enough to melt by aluminium-26 | Formation does not track individual planetesimals' formation times | Melting from each planetesimal's formation time and size (26Al heating) | Stand-in; tuned |
| SB-core-keeps | pack `makeup.core_keeps` 0.9 | The largest piece of a shattered world takes 90% of its metal | Formation spreads a shattered world over a debris cloud by mass only | A fragment model for differentiated targets (Asphaug 2010; Marcus 2009 mantle stripping) | Stand-in |

| COMET-tails | renderer comet.ts `cometTails`, `ION_WIDTH` 0.25, `TAIL_GRAINS` 900 | Both tails drawn straight away from the star at the kernel's lengths; the dust tail as wide as the coma, the ion tail a quarter of that; grains spaced as 1/s along the tail (a steady flow); the ion tail coloured by CO+ (426 nm) or, for a water comet, H2O+ (615 nm) | The kernel gives tail lengths but not widths, grain sizes (β) or the ions' mix, and the line list has no cometary ion lines yet | A dust tail curved by each grain's β and age (synchrones and syndynes, Finson and Probstein 1968); widths from the outflow speed; ion lines from the light thread's line list | Stand-in |
| SB-ring-viscosity | renderer ring-structure.ts `RING_VISCOSITY_M2_S` 2e-3 | One effective viscosity for every ring | Rings carry no viscosity or particle size yet | Viscosity from optical depth, particle size and self-gravity wakes (Daisaka et al. 2001), per ring | Stand-in; inside Tajeddine et al. 2017's 1 to 3e-3 m^2/s for the outer A and B rings |
| SB-resonance-j2 | ring-structure.ts `ringMarks`, `EDGE_SLACK` 0.01 | Resonances placed by Kepler's law alone, without the planet's oblateness (Mimas 2:1 about 0.6% inside the B ring's edge); a recorded edge within 1% of a resonance is kept | The planet's J2 is not on the ring data | Resonance condition with the precession rates from J2 and J4 | Stand-in |
| SB-resonance-dip | ring-structure.ts | A resonance weaker than the viscosity is drawn as a band of half-width r sqrt(m mu) whose optical depth drops by ratio/(1 + ratio); density waves (Tiscareno et al. 2007 eq. 5) are not drawn | The 1D profile has no room for a wave train yet; the surface density is not on the ring data | The density-wave train from the surface density | Drawn from the numbers |
| SB-gap-width | ring-structure.ts `GAP_PER_ROOT_MU` 415 | An embedded moon's gap half-width = 415 sqrt(mu) a, fitted to the Encke gap (Pan, 325 km) and the Keeler gap (Daphnis, 42 km); both fit within 2% | The two gaps measured; a torque-balance gap needs the viscosity and the moon's eccentricity | Gap from the moon's torque against viscous spreading (Lissauer, Shu and Cuzzi 1981) | Stand-in (fitted to two gaps) |
| SB-comet-balance | sse-small comet.rs, pack `comet` | Each ice sublimates freely from its own share of an active fraction (5%) of the surface, in balance with a quarter of the subsolar sunlight (a fast-turning nucleus); the onset distance uses the subsolar point; Bond albedo = 0.3 x geometric | No thermal model of the nucleus (no heat flow inward, no day-night cycle) | A 1D thermal model with conduction, porosity and a dust mantle (Prialnik et al. 2004) | Stand-in |
| SB-comet-mantle | pack `comet.mantle_factor` 0.1 | CO2 and CO leave with the water they are trapped in once water is past its onset, in proportion to their shares; otherwise they sublimate from under the mantle at a tenth of the bare rate | Trapping and burial depths are not modelled | Release from amorphous ice and from depth | Stand-in; not fitted |
| SB-comet-coma | pack `comet` outflow 0.85 km/s r^-0.5 (the lifetimes are on the data sheet, `photochemistry.lifetime_1au`), dust to gas 1, tail age 10 days, beta 0.5, solar wind 400 km/s | Coma = the leading gas's scale length (speed x photodissociation lifetime); dust tail = how far radiation pressure carries beta-0.5 dust in 10 days; ion tail = how far the 400 km/s solar wind carries ions in the same 10 days (both tails are what the star carried off over one tail age; ions do not start at wind speed, so this is an upper bound). Far from the star the coma grows while the gas thins: drawing fades it by production | Survey values recalled, not read | A Haser coma and a syndyne and synchrone dust tail model (Finson and Probstein 1968) | Drawn from the numbers; values not checked |
| SB-comet-surface-ice | sse-small comet.rs `keeps_surface_ice`, pack `ice.survives_below_k` 150 K | Only a member whose blackbody temperature (at the distance it spends its time) is below 150 K still has ice at its surface to wake; a GM-made comet nucleus is a fresh arrival and keeps its ice. The same rule the type label reads | A sharp line, one ice (water) for all three; no buried ice dug up by impacts (main-belt comets), no loss time worked out from the sublimation rate | Ice loss over the body's time on its orbit from the energy balance, with burial depth | Stand-in |
